```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-Life CaffeineDecayRule
//

import Foundation

/// The decay model's business rule: it turns caffeine intakes and a half-life into caffeine levels.
///
/// Each intake decays exponentially from the moment it's consumed (first-order elimination with instant
/// absorption), and the level at any moment is the sum of the intakes that still count (superposition). An intake
/// stops counting once it's past its peak and below ``negligibleMilligrams``.
///
/// The rule holds no state and reads no clock, so the current time is always an input. Repositories execute it.
/// See the Caffeine Decay Model article for the math and the requirements it meets.
nonisolated struct CaffeineDecayRule: Sendable {
    /// The amount below which an intake past its peak stops counting, in milligrams.
    static let negligibleMilligrams = 0.5
    /// The time between the curve's samples, in seconds.
    static let sampleSpacing: TimeInterval = 60
    /// How far the curve reaches either side of the current minute, in seconds.
    static let windowRadius: TimeInterval = 12 * 60 * 60
    /// The number of levels in a curve: 1,440 for a 24-hour window at one-minute spacing.
    static let sampleCount = Int(2 * windowRadius / sampleSpacing)

    /// Returns the caffeine in the body at `date`: the sum of every intake that still counts at that moment.
    ///
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - date: The moment to evaluate.
    ///   - intakes: The intakes to sum. Intakes consumed after `date` add nothing.
    ///   - halfLife: The half-life to decay each intake with.
    func level(at date: Date, from intakes: [CaffeineIntake], halfLife: CaffeineHalfLife) -> CaffeineLevel {
        let milligrams = intakes.reduce(0.0) { total, intake in
            let remaining = remaining(of: intake, at: date, halfLife: halfLife)
            return remaining >= Self.negligibleMilligrams ? total + remaining : total
        }
        return CaffeineLevel(date: date, milligrams: milligrams)
    }

    /// Returns the curve for `now`: ``sampleCount`` levels, one per ``sampleSpacing``, from ``windowRadius`` before
    /// the current minute to ``windowRadius`` after it.
    ///
    /// The level at index `sampleCount / 2` is for the current minute.
    ///
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - intakes: The intakes to sum.
    ///   - halfLife: The half-life to decay each intake with.
    ///   - now: The current time.
    func curve(from intakes: [CaffeineIntake], halfLife: CaffeineHalfLife, now: Date) -> [CaffeineLevel] {
        let start = windowStart(for: now)
        return (0..<Self.sampleCount).map { index in
            let date = start.addingTimeInterval(Double(index) * Self.sampleSpacing)
            return level(at: date, from: intakes, halfLife: halfLife)
        }
    }

    /// Returns the intakes that are negligible at the window's first sample, so a repository can mark them.
    ///
    /// An intake is negligible once it's past its peak and below ``negligibleMilligrams``. Under instant absorption,
    /// its peak is the moment it's consumed. A negligible intake adds nothing anywhere in the curve for `now`.
    ///
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - intakes: The intakes to check.
    ///   - halfLife: The half-life to decay each intake with.
    ///   - now: The current time.
    func negligibleIntakes(
        from intakes: [CaffeineIntake], halfLife: CaffeineHalfLife, now: Date
    ) -> [CaffeineIntake] {
        let start = windowStart(for: now)
        return intakes.filter { intake in
            start >= intake.consumedAt
                && remaining(of: intake, at: start, halfLife: halfLife) < Self.negligibleMilligrams
        }
    }

    /// The window's first sample: the current minute, minus ``windowRadius``.
    private func windowStart(for now: Date) -> Date {
        let spacing = Self.sampleSpacing
        let currentMinute = (now.timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate / spacing).rounded(.down) * spacing
        return Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate: currentMinute - Self.windowRadius)
    }

    /// The caffeine left from `intake` at `date`, ignoring the negligible cutoff. It's zero before the intake.
    ///
    /// Uses the half-life form of the decay, `D · 2^(−t / T½)`, which is exact at whole half-lives.
    private func remaining(of intake: CaffeineIntake, at date: Date, halfLife: CaffeineHalfLife) -> Double {
        let elapsed = date.timeIntervalSince(intake.consumedAt)
        guard elapsed >= 0 else { return 0 }
        return intake.milligrams * exp2(-elapsed / halfLife.seconds)
    }
}

```
```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-LifeTests FakeCaffeineDecayRepository
//

import Foundation

@testable import Half_Life

/// An in-memory caffeine decay repository for use case tests.
///
/// It streams the curves it was given, then finishes, and it records every intake added to it.
actor FakeCaffeineDecayRepository: CaffeineDecayRepository {
    /// The curves that `curve()` streams, in order.
    let curves: [[CaffeineLevel]]
    /// The error `add(_:)` throws instead of recording the intake, if any.
    let addError: (any Error)?
    /// Every intake added so far, in order.
    private(set) var added: [CaffeineIntake] = []

    init(curves: [[CaffeineLevel]] = [], addError: (any Error)? = nil) {
        self.curves = curves
        self.addError = addError
    }

    nonisolated func curve() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]> {
        AsyncStream { continuation in
            for curve in curves {
                continuation.yield(curve)
            }
            continuation.finish()
        }
    }

    func add(_ intake: CaffeineIntake) async throws {
        if let addError {
            throw addError
        }
        added.append(intake)
    }
}
```

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |

//
// Half-LifeTests ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCaseTests
//

import Foundation
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

/// Checks that observing the curve streams exactly what the repository publishes.
struct ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCaseTests {

    @Test func streamsEveryCurveTheRepositoryPublishesInOrder() async {
        let midnight = Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate: 0)
        let first = [CaffeineLevel(date: midnight, milligrams: 128)]
        let second = [CaffeineLevel(date: midnight, milligrams: 192)]
        let observe = ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCase(repository: FakeCaffeineDecayRepository(curves: [first, second]))

        var received: [[CaffeineLevel]] = []
        for await curve in observe() {
            received.append(curve)
        }

        #expect(received == [first, second])
    }
}

```

```
import Foundation
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

/// Checks that logging an intake adds it through the repository, and that a failure reaches the caller.
struct LogCaffeineIntakeUseCaseTests {

    struct StoreFailed: Error {}

    let intake = CaffeineIntake(id: UUID(), milligrams: 128, consumedAt: Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate: 0))

    @Test func addsTheIntakeToTheRepository() async throws {
        let repository = FakeCaffeineDecayRepository()

        try await LogCaffeineIntakeUseCase(repository: repository)(intake)

        #expect(await repository.added == [intake])
    }

    @Test func throwsTheRepositorysError() async {
        let log = LogCaffeineIntakeUseCase(repository: FakeCaffeineDecayRepository(addError: StoreFailed()))

        await #expect(throws: StoreFailed.self) {
            try await log(intake)
        }
    }
}

```
```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-Life CaffeineDecayRepository
//

/// The source of truth for the active caffeine curve.
///
/// An implementation stores intakes through an intake data source, reads the half-life from a half-life data
/// source, and executes ``CaffeineDecayRule`` to calculate the curve. It has the intake data source mark the
/// intakes the rule finds negligible, so later fetches skip them. The Caffeine Decay Model article lists its
/// requirements, REPO-1 to REPO-6.
nonisolated protocol CaffeineDecayRepository: Sendable {
    /// Streams the active curve.
    ///
    /// Each new subscriber immediately receives a curve calculated for the current time. After that, every
    /// subscriber receives a new curve whenever the data the curve comes from changes. The repository never
    /// recalculates on a timer.
    func curve() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]>

    /// Stores a new intake, then publishes a recalculated curve to every subscriber.
    ///
    /// - Parameter intake: The intake to store.
    /// - Throws: An error if the intake couldn't be stored.
    func add(_ intake: CaffeineIntake) async throws
}

```

```
//
// Half-Life ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCase
//

/// Streams the active caffeine curve, starting with one calculated for the current time.
nonisolated struct ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCase: Sendable {
    /// The repository that owns the curve.
    let repository: any CaffeineDecayRepository

    /// Returns a stream of the active curve: one calculated for the current time, then a new one after every
    /// change to the data it comes from.
    func callAsFunction() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]> {
        repository.curve()
    }
}

```

```
//

/// Adds a caffeine intake. The repository then publishes a recalculated curve.
nonisolated struct LogCaffeineIntakeUseCase: Sendable {
    /// The repository that stores the intake.
    let repository: any CaffeineDecayRepository

    /// Adds `intake` through the repository.
    ///
    /// - Parameter intake: The intake to add.
    /// - Throws: The repository's error if the intake couldn't be stored.
    func callAsFunction(_ intake: CaffeineIntake) async throws {
        try await repository.add(intake)
    }
}
```

```
//
// Half-LifeTests FakeCaffeineDecayRepository

import Foundation

@testable import Half_Life

/// An in-memory caffeine decay repository for use case tests.
///
/// It streams the curves it was given, then finishes, and it records every intake added to it. Like the Domain
/// types, it's isolated to the main actor.
@MainActor
final class FakeCaffeineDecayRepository: CaffeineDecayRepository {
    /// The curves that `curve()` streams, in order.
    let curves: [[CaffeineLevel]]
    /// The error `add(_:)` throws instead of recording the intake, if any.
    let addError: (any Error)?
    /// Every intake added so far, in order.
    private(set) var added: [CaffeineIntake] = []

    init(curves: [[CaffeineLevel]] = [], addError: (any Error)? = nil) {
        self.curves = curves
        self.addError = addError
    }

    func curve() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]> {
        let curves = curves
        return AsyncStream { continuation in
            for curve in curves {
                continuation.yield(curve)
            }
            continuation.finish()
        }
    }

    func add(_ intake: CaffeineIntake) async throws {
        if let addError {
            throw addError
        }
        added.append(intake)
    }
}

```

- `replace_all`: `false`

- `old_string`: ``Domain types take the app target's default `MainActor` isolation. Work moves off the main actor only explicitly and intentionally (constitution Article IV.1). Because the entities' conformances are `MainActor`-isolated, a repository that uses them is isolated to the main actor too (Article I.13 allows a single global actor), and Domain tests are `@MainActor`.``
- `new_string`:

```
Only presentation, meaning reducers and views, runs on the main actor.

- **Domain types are declared `nonisolated`.** Entities are immutable `Sendable` values, so each actor works on its own copy and there's no shared mutable state to protect. Business rules and use cases hold no state.
- **Repositories will be their own actors** (Article I.13). A repository executes business rules on its own actor and hands entities to presentation.
- **Every opt-out is explicit.** The app target still defaults to `MainActor`, so each Domain and Data declaration opts out explicitly (Article IV.1). Whether to change the target's default is part of the open question below.
```

```
843	- **Interactions:**
844	  - `21:52` Asked for the drink composer's entities and repository in a catalog entry.
845	  - `21:58` Two mid-task messages. The entity holds a drink type (an enum), a quantity (shots for coffee, cups otherwise), a timestamp, and the total caffeine, estimated as the drink's caffeine per unit × quantity. Asked for research on caffeinated drinks, with an enum case for each.
846	  - `22:03` Mid-task, replaced the prototype's "Build it" composer with a scrollable list of drinks. One-tap favourites show the three or four most common drinks. Asked for an icon for each drink. The time is the AI's clock reading when it saw the message.
847	- **AI contribution:**
848	  - Added the `Drink Composer` article:
849	    - A table mapping each prototype element to an entity, to presentation, or to a cut. "Oat" is cut because milk doesn't change caffeine.
850	    - Entities: `DrinkType` (13 cases, with unit, caffeine per unit, and default quantity), `ServingUnit` (shot, cup, can), and `LoggedDrink` (`id`, `type`, `quantity`, `milligrams`, `consumedAt`). `LoggedDrink` projects to a `CaffeineIntake` with the same `id`.
851	    - `DrinkLogRepository` (publishes the whole log, stores before it publishes) and `LogDrinkUseCase`, which all three entry points call.
852	    - Iconography, privacy and logging, and requirements TYPE-1–4, DRINK-1, USE-1–3, and DLOG-1–3.
853	  - Researched the caffeine estimates:

855	    - Canned drinks: the makers' declared amounts, from coca-cola.com and redbull.com.
856	    - Spreads: the FDA's "Spilling the Beans" page. Its black-tea figure matches USDA's.
857	    - Matcha: 18.9–44.4 mg/g, from the Kochman et al. review on PMC.
858	    - Cold brew: Starbucks' 205 mg per Grande, through Caffeine Informer, because starbucks.com's page didn't render.
```

```
// Half-LifeTests UseCaseTesting
//

@testable import Half_Life

/// Runs `useCase` through the `UseCase` protocol. A use case that doesn't follow the pattern in constitution
/// Article I.7 fails to compile in its tests.
func executeThroughProtocol<U: UseCase>(_ useCase: U, _ input: U.Input) async throws -> U.Output {
    try await useCase.execute(input)
}
```

- `old_string`:

````
```swift
/// Streams every recorded caffeine entry, starting with the current set.
struct ObserveCaffeineEntriesUseCase: Sendable {
    let repository: any CaffeineRepository

    func callAsFunction() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineEntry]> {
        repository.entries()
    }
}
```
````
- `new_string`:

````
Every use case conforms to ``UseCase`` and exposes its one operation as `execute(_:)` (constitution Article I.7). The operation takes one `Input` and returns one `Output`, and either can be `Void`.

```swift
/// Streams the active caffeine curve, starting with one calculated for the current time.
nonisolated struct ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCase: UseCase {
    let repository: any CaffeineDecayRepository

    func execute(_ input: Void) -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]> {
        repository.curve()
    }
}
```

The protocol requires `async throws`. A use case that neither suspends nor throws, like this one, can leave both out, and a caller using the concrete type then needs no `try await`. Code that goes through the protocol, such as a generic test helper, still writes `try await`.
````

**22:17:38 · Tool result**

````
- **It publishes the whole log.** Features and use cases narrow it: Today (rank 4) shows today's drinks, one-tap favourites count the most common ones, and Patterns (rank 15) reads weeks at a time.
- **It stores before it publishes.** If the data source fails to store a drink, `log(_:)` throws and nothing is published. The feature finds out from the thrown error, and the log never shows a drink that wasn't saved.
- **Deleting and editing come later.** Delete + undo (rank 20) and retroactive timing (rank 17) add their own methods when they're built (constitution Article III.1).

### LogDrinkUseCase

The single use case that all three entry points call. Logging a drink is one business operation (constitution Article I.7), and it spans two repositories.

```swift
/// Records a drink the user consumed, and adds its caffeine to the curve.
nonisolated struct LogDrinkUseCase: Sendable {
    let drinkLog: any DrinkLogRepository
    let caffeineDecay: any CaffeineDecayRepository

    func callAsFunction(_ type: DrinkType, quantity: Int, consumedAt: Date) async throws
}
```

1. It creates the `LoggedDrink`: a new `id`, `milligrams` from `type.estimatedMilligrams(quantity:)`, and the given `consumedAt`.
2. It records the drink with `DrinkLogRepository.log(_:)`.
3. It adds the drink's `intake` with `CaffeineDecayRepository.add(_:)`, which recalculates and publishes the curve (REPO-5 in <doc:CaffeineDecayModel>).

Each repository keeps its own record. The drink log stores the drink. The decay repository stores the intake and, later, its negligible mark. The shared `id` links the two. The use case calls the repositories directly, because use cases depend on repository protocols (constitution Article I.9), so it doesn't go through `LogCaffeineIntakeUseCase`.

It rejects a quantity below 1 and a `consumedAt` later than the current time, by throwing before either repository is called. The clock and the ID generator are injected, so tests are deterministic.

### Entry points

| Entry point | Drink type | Quantity | Consumed at |
|-------------|------------|----------|-------------|
| Composer (rank 2) | The row picked from the list | Starts at the drink's `defaultQuantity`, and a stepper adjusts it | Now, or the "When" choice |
| One-tap favourite (rank 3) | One of the three or four most common drinks | The quantity it's most often logged at | Now |
| App Intent or Siri (rank 14) | An `AppEnum` parameter that mirrors `DrinkType` | Optional, defaulting to the drink's `defaultQuantity` | Now |

An App Intent calls the use case directly, without a reducer. How its `@Dependency` reaches the same app-scoped repository is designed with App Intents.
````

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-Life UseCase
//

/// A single business operation, run with ``execute(_:)`` (constitution Article I.7).
///
/// Every use case conforms to this protocol. It performs exactly one operation, holds nothing but references to
/// the repositories it uses, and lives only as long as the feature that owns it.
nonisolated protocol UseCase: Sendable {
    /// The operation's input. It's `Void` for an operation that takes none.
    associatedtype Input: Sendable
    /// The operation's result. It's `Void` for an operation that returns nothing.
    associatedtype Output: Sendable

    /// Performs the operation.
    ///
    /// A conforming type whose operation neither suspends nor throws can leave out `async` or `throws`. Callers
    /// that use the concrete type then need no `try await`.
    ///
    /// - Parameter input: The operation's input.
    /// - Returns: The operation's result.
    func execute(_ input: Input) async throws -> Output
}

```

- **The worked examples are the test values.** The rule is tested against the article's worked examples, one test per requirement (RULE-1 to RULE-7).
- **The 0.5 mg boundary is exact.** The rule calculates with the half-life form, so 128 mg after exactly 44 h comes out at exactly 0.5 mg.
- **Use-case tests go through the protocol.** A use case that drifts from the pattern fails to compile.

**Isolation.** I went back and forth on this during the task, so here is where it ended: only presentation runs on the main actor. Domain types are declared `nonisolated`, explicitly, as Article IV.1 requires. Future repositories will be their own actors. The Architecture article records this.

Entities can't be "isolated" to an actor in Swift's sense, because they're handed from a repository's actor to the main actor. They're safe because they're immutable `Sendable` values: each actor gets its own copy.

**22:32:22 · Tool result**

```
1	# Half-Life Constitution
2	
3	> **Status: DRAFT** — starter version for review. Edit, remove, or add principles, then change the status to **Ratified** with the date.
4	
5	This document defines the foundational principles every change to Half-Life must follow. It overrides convenience, existing code patterns, and any other project guidance (including `CLAUDE.md`). If a change can't comply with it, the change is wrong, or this document needs a deliberate amendment. It is never quietly worked around.
6	
7	## Article I: Architecture
8	
9	The app combines **The Composable Architecture (TCA)** ([pointfreeco/swift-composable-architecture](https://github.com/pointfreeco/swift-composable-architecture)) with **Clean Architecture**. It has three layers.
10	
11	| Layer | Contains | May depend on |
12	|-------|----------|---------------|
13	| **Presentation** | TCA features: reducers and their views | Domain (use cases and entities) |
14	| **Domain** | Entities, use cases, and repository protocols | Nothing (Swift and Foundation only) |
15	| **Data** | Repository implementations and data sources (HealthKit, SwiftData, …) | Domain (to implement its protocols) |
16	
17	Dependencies point inward, toward Domain. Domain never imports SwiftUI, ComposableArchitecture, HealthKit, or SwiftData. Nothing depends on Presentation.
18	
19	### Presentation: TCA features
20	
21	1. **Features are reducers.** Each feature is a `@Reducer` with an `@ObservableState` `State`, an `Action` enum, and a `body`. Presentation logic lives in reducers, never in views.
22	2. **Views are thin.** A view renders from a `StoreOf<Feature>` and sends actions. It holds no business state of its own (`@State` only for purely visual, ephemeral concerns).
23	3. **Actions call use cases.** A reducer reaches business logic and data only through use cases, injected with `@Dependency`, and calls them from `Effect`s. It never touches a repository or data source directly.
24	4. **Repository data is reduced into State.** A feature subscribes to repository data through an `Observe…` use case in a `.run` effect. The feature sends one action per emitted value (e.g. `.entriesUpdated([CaffeineEntry])`), and the reducer reduces that value into `State`. The subscription is tied to the feature's lifetime: the view starts it from `.task { await store.send(.task).finish() }`, or it is cancelled by ID when the feature is dismissed.
25	5. **One-way data flow.** Commands flow action → use case → repository. Updates flow repository → `Observe…` use case → action → reducer → `State`. A reducer does not optimistically write repository-owned data into `State`; it waits for the repository to emit. Any documented exception must be recorded in the DocC catalog.
26	6. **Composition and navigation.** Features compose with `Scope`, `.ifLet`, and `.forEach`. Navigation is state-driven using `@Presents` / `PresentationAction` and `StackState` / `StackAction`. These rules govern the app's own screens. System UI that a framework provides, such as MessageUI's Mail composer or HealthKit's authorization sheet, is presented by the data source that wraps the framework (I.14). The feature learns the outcome through the repository, like any other data.
27	
28	### Domain: use cases and entities
29	
30	7. **Use cases are single-purpose.** Each use case performs exactly one business operation. It conforms to the Domain's `UseCase` protocol and exposes the operation as `execute(_:)`, which takes one `Input` and returns one `Output` (`Void` when there's none). It is named for that operation: `LogCaffeineIntakeUseCase`, `ObserveCaffeineEntriesUseCase`.
31	8. **Use cases are lifetime-scoped.** A use case lives only as long as the feature that owns it. It holds no state of its own beyond references to the repositories it uses; long-lived state belongs in repositories, and per-screen state belongs in feature `State`. Use cases never cache data between calls.
32	9. **Use cases depend on repository protocols**, which Domain defines. They never depend on concrete implementations.
33	10. **Entities** are plain `Sendable`, `Equatable` value types with no framework dependencies.
34	
35	### Data: repositories and data sources
36	
37	11. **Repositories hold state.** A repository is the single source of truth for its data. It is app-scoped, created once, and outlives every feature. It holds the current data in memory and persists it through data sources.
38	12. **Repositories publish changes.** Every repository exposes its data as an `AsyncStream` that emits the current value immediately on subscription and then every change.
39	13. **Repositories are concurrency-safe:** an `actor`, or isolated to a single global actor.
40	14. **Data sources wrap frameworks.** Data sources are the only code that touches HealthKit, SwiftData, the file system, or other system frameworks. Repositories talk to data sources, never to frameworks directly.
```

- `content`:

```
import ComposableArchitecture
import SwiftUI

/// Experiment variant A: an explicitly main-actor reducer under a nonisolated target default.
@MainActor
@Reducer
struct CounterFeature {
    /// The counter's state.
    @ObservableState
    struct State: Equatable {
        /// The count.
        var count = 0
    }

    /// The counter's actions.
    enum Action {
        case incrementTapped
        case task
        case loaded(Int)
    }

    /// The reducer's body.
    var body: some ReducerOf<Self> {
        Reduce { state, action in
            switch action {
            case .incrementTapped:
                state.count += 1
                return .none
            case .task:
                return .run { send in
                    await send(.loaded(5))
                }
            case let .loaded(count):
                state.count = count
                return .none
            }
        }
    }
}

/// The counter's view.
@MainActor
struct CounterView: View {
    /// The store.
    let store: StoreOf<CounterFeature>

    /// The body.
    var body: some View {
        Button("\(store.count)") { store.send(.incrementTapped) }
    }
}

```

```
import ComposableArchitecture
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

@MainActor
struct CounterFeatureTests {
    @Test func increments() async {
        let store = TestStore(initialState: CounterFeature.State()) { CounterFeature() }
        await store.send(.incrementTapped) { $0.count = 1 }
    }

    @Test func loadsThroughAnEffect() async {
        let store = TestStore(initialState: CounterFeature.State()) { CounterFeature() }
        await store.send(.task)
        await store.receive(\.loaded) { $0.count = 5 }
    }
}
```

**22:38:29 · Tool call: Edit**

- `old_string`: ``1. Concurrency follows Swift's data-race safety model. The project defaults to `MainActor` isolation, and work moves off the main actor explicitly and intentionally.``
- `new_string`:

```
1. Concurrency follows Swift's data-race safety model. The app target's default actor isolation is `nonisolated`. Only the UI and presentation layers run on the main actor, and they declare their isolation explicitly.
   1. **UI types are `@MainActor`.** Views, the app, and other types that conform to a SwiftUI or UIKit UI protocol are marked `@MainActor` explicitly, even where the protocol would infer it.
   2. **Reducers are `nonisolated`.** Each `@Reducer` type is marked `nonisolated` explicitly, and is never `@MainActor`. TCA runs reducers on the store, which is `@MainActor`. A `@MainActor` reducer crashes at runtime (verified 2026-09-11 against TCA 1.26.2).
   3. **Domain and Data stay off the main actor.** Domain types are `nonisolated`: entities are immutable `Sendable` values, and business rules and use cases hold no state. Repositories are their own actors (Article I.13). A data source that presents system UI (Article I.6) runs only that presentation on the main actor, in a method marked `@MainActor`.
   4. **Enforcement.** SwiftLint custom rules in `.swiftlint.yml` reject a UI type without `@MainActor`, a reducer without `nonisolated`, and a `@MainActor` reducer.
```

- `old_string`:

```
### Open issue: actor isolation vs. TCA (undecided, don't guess)

The app target sets `SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor`, and TCA does not work cleanly with it. This was verified on 2026-09-11 against TCA 1.26.2 in a scratch copy of the project:

- **As-is:** reducers crash at runtime with infinite `reduce`/`_reduce` recursion.
- **Marking reducers and their `State`/`Action` `nonisolated`:** tests pass, but TCA's macro-generated conformances produce warnings, which become errors in Swift 6 mode. That violates Article IX.
- **Switching the app target to a `nonisolated` default:** builds with zero warnings, and tests pass.

The user has deferred this decision. **Do not write the first TCA feature until the user settles it.** Raise it as soon as TCA work is requested.
```
- `new_string`:

```
### Actor isolation (decided 2026-09-11, constitution Article IV.1)

The app target's default actor isolation is `nonisolated`. Only UI and presentation run on the main actor, and they say so explicitly:

- **Views, the app, and other UI types:** `@MainActor`, written out even where the protocol would infer it.
- **`@Reducer` types:** `nonisolated`, written out, and never `@MainActor`.
- **Domain:** `nonisolated`. **Repositories:** their own actors, off the main actor.

SwiftLint enforces the UI and reducer rules. The evidence comes from scratch copies of the project, tested against TCA 1.26.2 on 2026-09-11:

| Target default | Reducer | Result |
|----------------|---------|--------|
| `MainActor` | Unannotated | Reducers crash at runtime with infinite `reduce`/`_reduce` recursion |
| `MainActor` | `nonisolated`, with its `State`/`Action` | Tests pass, but TCA's macro-generated conformances produce warnings |
| `nonisolated` | `@MainActor` | The `TestStore` tests crash |
| `nonisolated` | `nonisolated`, with a `@MainActor` view | Zero warnings. Tests pass, including an effect that sends an action back |
```

```
38-- `ai_log.md` is the timestamped log of **every** AI interaction on this project. Questions, reviews, debugging, and planning count too, not just changes. Its **Rules** section defines the format; read it before finishing any task.
39-- Log every task, including ones that change no files. A task is not finished until its entry is appended, and for a change the entry goes in the same commit.
40-- Take every timestamp from the clock with `date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M %z'`. Run it when you receive the prompt that starts a task, at each later prompt or decision, and when the task ends. Never estimate a time afterwards; if one must be reconstructed, mark it `~` and say from what.
```

```
=== Architecture isolation section ===
30:Only presentation, meaning stores and views, runs on the main actor. The app target's default actor isolation is `nonisolated` (constitution Article IV.1), because TCA reducers crash under a `MainActor` default, and marking TCA types `nonisolated` under that default leaves compiler warnings.
31-
32-- **Main-actor code says so.** SwiftUI views are isolated to the main actor through `View`, and TCA's `Store` is `@MainActor`. Anything else that must run there is marked `@MainActor`.
33-- **Domain types are immutable `Sendable` values.** Each actor works on its own copy, so there's no shared mutable state to protect. Business rules and use cases hold no state. Domain declarations written before the default changed still say `nonisolated`. With the new default, that's redundant but harmless.
34-- **Repositories are their own actors** (Article I.13). A repository executes business rules on its own actor and hands entities to presentation.
```

**22:40:58 · Tool call: Edit**

- `replace_all`: `false`
- `file_path`: `~/Development/Femmli/Half-Life/constitution.md`
- `old_string`: ``1. Concurrency follows Swift's data-race safety model. The app target's default actor isolation is `nonisolated`, because TCA reducers fail under a `MainActor` default. Only presentation runs on the main actor: SwiftUI views are isolated to it through `View`, and any other code that must run there is marked `@MainActor` explicitly. Repositories are actors (I.13), and Domain values are immutable and `Sendable`.``
- `new_string`:

```
1. Concurrency follows Swift's data-race safety model. The app target's default actor isolation is `nonisolated`, because TCA reducers fail under a `MainActor` default. Only the UI and presentation layers run on the main actor, and every UI and presentation type states its isolation explicitly.
   1. **UI types are `@MainActor`.** Views, the app, and other types that conform to a SwiftUI or UIKit UI protocol are marked `@MainActor` explicitly, even where the protocol, such as `View`, would infer it.
   2. **Reducers are `nonisolated`.** A reducer reduces `Sendable` state and actions, and TCA runs it on the store, which is `@MainActor`. So each `@Reducer` type is marked `nonisolated` explicitly, and is never `@MainActor`: a `@MainActor` reducer crashes at runtime (verified 2026-09-11 against TCA 1.26.2).
   3. **Domain and Data stay off the main actor.** Domain types are `nonisolated`: entities are immutable `Sendable` values, and business rules and use cases hold no state. Repositories are their own actors (Article I.13). A data source that presents system UI (Article I.6) runs only that presentation on the main actor, in a method marked `@MainActor`.
   4. **Enforcement.** SwiftLint custom rules in `.swiftlint.yml` reject a UI type without `@MainActor`, a reducer without `nonisolated`, and a `@MainActor` reducer.
```

- `old_string`:

```
The app target has no `SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION` setting, so its default isolation is `nonisolated`. Don't add the setting back. Verified on 2026-09-11 against TCA 1.26.2:

- **A `MainActor` default:** reducers crash at runtime with infinite `reduce`/`_reduce` recursion.
- **A `MainActor` default with reducers and their `State`/`Action` marked `nonisolated`:** TCA's macro-generated conformances produce warnings, which violates Article IX.
- **A `nonisolated` default:** builds with zero warnings, and tests pass.

Only presentation runs on the main actor. SwiftUI views get it from `View`. Any other code that must run there is marked `@MainActor` explicitly.
```
- `new_string`:

```
The app target has no `SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION` setting, so its default isolation is `nonisolated`. Don't add the setting back.

Only UI and presentation run on the main actor, and they state their isolation explicitly:

- **Views, the app, and other UI types:** `@MainActor`, on the line directly above the declaration, even where `View` or `App` would infer it.
- **`@Reducer` types:** `nonisolated`, written out (`@Reducer nonisolated struct …`), and never `@MainActor`. A reducer reduces `Sendable` state, and TCA runs it on the `@MainActor` store.
- **Domain:** `nonisolated`. **Repositories:** their own actors, off the main actor.

SwiftLint enforces the UI and reducer rules. The evidence comes from scratch copies of the project, tested against TCA 1.26.2 on 2026-09-11:

| Target default | Reducer | Result |
|----------------|---------|--------|
| `MainActor` | Unannotated | Reducers crash at runtime with infinite `reduce`/`_reduce` recursion |
| `MainActor` | `nonisolated`, with its `State`/`Action` | TCA's macro-generated conformances produce warnings (Article IX) |
| `nonisolated` | `@MainActor` | The `TestStore` tests crash |
| `nonisolated` | `nonisolated` struct and enum reducers, with a `@MainActor` view | No isolation warnings. Tests pass, including an effect that sends an action back |
```

Both constitution gaps are amended, and the new isolation rules are written down, enforced by SwiftLint, and applied to the existing UI code. I couldn't get a full test run on the live tree, though, because other sessions' tests don't compile right now.

**Reducers are explicitly `nonisolated`, not `@MainActor`.** I tested this first in a scratch copy of the project, and it confirms your point:

| Reducer, under a `nonisolated` default | Result |
|---|---|
| `@MainActor @Reducer` | The tests crash at runtime |
| `@Reducer nonisolated struct`, with a `@MainActor` view | Tests pass with zero warnings, including an effect that sends an action back |
| `@Reducer nonisolated enum` | Tests pass, with no isolation warnings |

**The amendments:**
- **Business rules:** Article I's layer table and item I.10 now include them.
- **Repositories:** I.13 keeps them off the main actor.
- **Isolation (IV.1):** now has four sub-rules:
  - UI types say `@MainActor` explicitly, even where `View` or `App` would infer it.
  - Reducers say `nonisolated` explicitly and are never `@MainActor`.
  - Domain and Data stay off the main actor. A data source runs only its system-UI presentation, such as the Mail composer, on the main actor.
  - SwiftLint enforces the UI and reducer rules.

Both amendments are recorded in the Amendments table. `CLAUDE.md` has the decided isolation section with the evidence table, plus a "State isolation explicitly" bullet, and the Architecture article matches.

**Someone else had already started this change.** The app target's isolation setting was already removed, making the default `nonisolated`. `CLAUDE.md`, Article IV.1, and the Architecture article had also been rewritten, and an Amendments row added. No log entry claims those edits, so it may have been you or another session. That version said views get `@MainActor` implicitly "through `View`", which contradicts your explicit-declarations rule. I replaced that wording, kept its reasoning and "don't add the setting back", and ordered the two Article IV amendment rows oldest first.

**Enforcement:** three new SwiftLint rules. On sample files they catch all 7 intended violations, with no false alarms on the compliant samples. On the project they flagged `ContentView` and `Half_LifeApp`, which now have `@MainActor`. The rules have two known limits: they don't look inside `extension` conformances, and the reducer rule can be fooled if another attribute sits between `@Reducer` and the declaration.

```
/// The source of truth for the active caffeine curve.
///
/// An implementation stores intakes through an intake data source, reads the half-life from a half-life data
/// source, and executes ``CaffeineDecayRule`` to calculate the curve. It has the intake data source mark the
/// intakes the rule finds negligible, so later fetches skip them. The Caffeine Decay Model article lists its
/// requirements, REPO-1 to REPO-6.
nonisolated protocol CaffeineDecayRepository: Sendable {
    /// Streams the active curve.
    ///
    /// Each new subscriber immediately receives a curve calculated for the current time. After that, every
    /// subscriber receives a new curve whenever the data the curve comes from changes. The repository never
    /// recalculates on a timer.
    func curve() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]>

    /// Stores a new intake, then publishes a recalculated curve to every subscriber.
    ///
    /// - Parameter intake: The intake to store.
    /// - Throws: An error if the intake couldn't be stored.
    func add(_ intake: CaffeineIntake) async throws
}
=== LoggedDrink ===
import Foundation

/// One drink the user consumed.
///
/// A logged drink keeps the caffeine it was logged with. If a later version corrects a drink's caffeine per unit,
/// drinks already logged keep their amounts, so history and past curves don't change. See the Drink Composer
/// article.
struct LoggedDrink: Identifiable, Equatable, Sendable {
    /// Identifies the drink, so it can be listed, and later deleted or edited. Its intake shares it.
    let id: UUID
    /// Which drink.
    let type: DrinkType
    /// How many units of the drink's ``DrinkType/unit``.
    let quantity: Int
    /// The total estimated caffeine, in milligrams. Stored when the drink is logged, never recalculated.
    let milligrams: Double
    /// When the drink was consumed. It can be earlier than when it was logged.
    let consumedAt: Date
```

````
An App Intent calls the use case directly, without a reducer. How its `@Dependency` reaches the same app-scoped repository is designed with App Intents.

## Data flow

A drink is stored once, written in one place, and read by two repositories.

```
 Command:  Row + stepper ─▶ Action ─▶ Reducer ─▶ LogDrinkUseCase ─▶ DrinkLogRepository.log ─▶ DrinkDataSource.store
 Update:   DrinkDataSource change ─┬─▶ DrinkLogRepository: re-read drinks ─▶ AsyncStream<[LoggedDrink]>
                                   └─▶ CaffeineDecayRepository: re-read active intakes ─▶ rule ─▶ AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]>
```

- **One write.** The use case stores the drink through one repository. Nothing writes a second time just to tell the curve.
- **The data source informs.** The drink data source is the only code that stores drinks, so it knows when they change. After every successful store, it signals a change to every repository subscribed to it. Each repository re-reads what it needs through its own query. `DrinkLogRepository` reads every drink. `CaffeineDecayRepository` reads the intakes of drinks that aren't marked negligible (REPO-1 in <doc:CaffeineDecayModel>).
- **Repositories subscribe for their lifetime.** Each app-scoped repository subscribes when it's created, and keeps the subscription for the life of the app. Features still observe repositories only through `Observe…` use cases (constitution Article I.4).
- **Marks don't signal.** When the decay repository has the data source mark negligible intakes, no change is signalled. A mark changes nothing that any repository publishes (REPO-4), so a signal would only make the decay repository recalculate for nothing.
- **Later writers are covered.** Delete + undo (rank 20), retroactive timing (rank 17), and the demo seed (rank 8) write through the same data source, so the log and the curve follow them with no extra wiring.
- **A failure has one place to happen.** If storing fails, `log(_:)` throws, no change is signalled, and neither repository publishes. The reducer logs the error's domain and code (constitution Article XI.6.4).

### DrinkDataSource

A Data-layer protocol. Its implementation is the only code that touches the storage framework (constitution Article I.14). It's the intake data source that <doc:CaffeineDecayModel> describes: the intakes it returns are projected from stored drinks.

| Operation | What it does |
|-----------|--------------|
| `store(_:)` | Stores a `LoggedDrink`, unmarked, then signals a change. |
| `drinks()` | Returns every stored drink, oldest first. |
| `activeIntakes()` | Returns the `intake` of every drink that isn't marked negligible. |
| `markNegligible(_:)` | Marks the given intakes negligible. It never clears a mark, deletes a drink, or signals a change. |
| `changes()` | Returns an `AsyncStream<Void>`, one per subscriber, that yields after each change. |

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| SRC-1 | Every subscriber to `changes()` gets one signal after each successful `store(_:)`. |
| SRC-2 | A failed `store(_:)` throws and signals nothing. |
| SRC-3 | `markNegligible(_:)` signals nothing. |
| SRC-4 | `activeIntakes()` returns each unmarked drink's `intake`, with the drink's `id`, and nothing for marked drinks. |
````

```
90:1. **On-device only.** User data never leaves the device. There is no backend and no iCloud sync, and the app makes no network request that carries user data.
96:   4. HealthKit data is never used for advertising or marketing, never shared with third parties, and never stored in iCloud.
98:4. **Storage.** Caffeine records are stored locally and protected with iOS Data Protection at `NSFileProtectionComplete` by default. A weaker class is used only when a feature needs background access, and that choice is documented in the catalog. Any credentials or secrets go in the Keychain.
=== ClockDataSource ===
import Foundation

/// The only source of the current time: the one place the app reads the system clock.
///
/// ``LiveCurrentTimeRepository`` reads it. The Today Screen article lists its requirements, CLOCK-1 to CLOCK-4,
/// and the Drink Composer article adds CLOCK-5.
protocol ClockDataSource: Sendable {
    /// Returns the current time.
    func now() -> Date

    /// Streams the current time as soon as it's subscribed to, then once at every whole minute.
    ///
    /// The stream finishes when its subscriber stops listening.
    func minutes() -> AsyncStream<Date>
}
=== TodayScreen status ===
| `CaffeineStatus` property | Type | Meaning |
|---------------------------|------|---------|
| `level` | ``CaffeineLevel`` | The caffeine in the body at the current minute |
| `activeIntakes` | `[CaffeineIntake]` | The intakes still counting at the current minute (the drinks still in your system) |
| `lastIntakeHalfGoneAt` | `Date?` | When the most recent intake is down to half its dose: its `consumedAt` plus the half-life. `nil` when that moment has passed or nothing is counting. |
| `levelAtBedtime` | ``CaffeineLevel``? | The level at the next bedtime |

- `status()` publishes a status as soon as it's subscribed to. After that, it publishes a new one at every whole minute from `ClockDataSource`, whenever the drink data source signals a change, and whenever the bedtime changes.
- The tips come from a Domain business rule that the repository executes, so no feature evaluates the decay function (VIEW-1 in <doc:CaffeineDecayModel>). The current level replaces VIEW-3's "found by date" lookup.
- "Half of your last cup is gone by" is exact under instant absorption. Bateman absorption (rank 5) changes the rule, not the entity.
- **The bedtime** belongs to the user's profile. Until onboarding (rank 6) stores one, it's a default of 10:30pm, the prototype's example. The Last Cup card's cutoff will share it. The repository reads it from the profile data source, which it shares with `UserProfileRepository`.
- **The next bedtime** is the first occurrence of the bedtime at or after the current minute. At 11:30pm, with a 10:30pm bedtime, that's tomorrow at 10:30pm. This is the AI's choice and still to be confirmed.

`CaffeineDecayFeature` observes the curve, the status, and the time of day. `TimeOfDayRepository` supplies the time shown beside the hero figure and the position of the curve's "now" marker.
```

```
=== LiveCurrentTimeRepository ===
import Foundation

/// The live current time repository. It reads the time from its injected ``ClockDataSource``.
///
/// It's an actor off the main actor, like every repository (constitution Article I.13). Its data is the clock, so
/// its stream publishes every minute on purpose. The Drink Composer article lists its requirements, TIME-1 and
/// TIME-2.
actor LiveCurrentTimeRepository: CurrentTimeRepository {
    private let dataSource: any ClockDataSource

    /// Creates the repository.
    ///
    /// - Parameter dataSource: The clock to read.
    init(dataSource: any ClockDataSource) {
        self.dataSource = dataSource
    }

    /// Returns the data source's current time.
    nonisolated func now() -> Date {
        dataSource.now()
    }

    /// Streams the data source's current time: once immediately, then at the start of each whole minute.
    nonisolated func currentTime() -> AsyncStream<Date> {
        dataSource.minutes()
    }
}
```

```
=== FakeClockDataSource ===
import Foundation

@testable import Half_Life

/// A clock data source that returns a fixed time and streams given minutes, for repository tests.
struct FakeClockDataSource: ClockDataSource {
    /// The date that `now()` returns.
    let date: Date
    /// The dates that `minutes()` streams, in order, before it finishes.
    let minuteDates: [Date]

    func now() -> Date {
        date
    }

    func minutes() -> AsyncStream<Date> {
        AsyncStream { continuation in
            for minute in minuteDates {
                continuation.yield(minute)
            }
            continuation.finish()
        }
    }
}
```

```
### Units

Amounts are `Double` milligrams and durations are `TimeInterval` seconds, with the unit in each property's name. The model is pure arithmetic on these values, and `Date` arithmetic already uses `TimeInterval`. Wrapping the values in `Measurement` would add a conversion to every calculation and gain nothing inside the Domain. The model's values are specified and tested to two decimal places, and the rule doesn't round them. Presentation converts to `Measurement` when it formats a value, and can round further for display, for example to whole milligrams. Everything users see stays locale-aware (constitution Article VII.3).

## Where the calculation lives

The decay function lives in the Domain layer as a **business rule**. That's a stateless type, `CaffeineDecayRule`, that turns intakes and a half-life into caffeine levels. It depends only on the entities above and on Foundation.

- **`CaffeineDecayRepository` executes the rule.** It fetches the intakes that aren't marked negligible from the intake data source, and reads the half-life from the half-life data source. Then it applies the rule for the current time, and publishes the resulting curve on its `AsyncStream`.
- **New intakes arrive from the data source.** Drinks are stored through `DrinkLogRepository` (see <doc:DrinkComposer>). The intake data source then signals a change to this repository, which re-fetches the intakes that aren't marked negligible, recalculates, and publishes the curve. Nothing adds an intake to this repository directly. `add(_:)` and `LogCaffeineIntakeUseCase` are built, but this flow supersedes them.
- **The half-life comes from a half-life data source.** The data source supplies `CaffeineHalfLife.standard` until the tuning features store a user's own value.
- **The curve covers a fixed window.** It runs from 12 hours before the current time to 12 hours after: 1,440 levels, one per minute. The window belongs to the Domain, not to presentation, because the rule decides negligibility at the window's first sample.
- **The repository marks intakes that no longer count.** An intake is negligible once it's past its peak and below 0.5 mg at the window's first sample. When the rule returns such intakes, the repository has the data source mark them, so later fetches skip them. A mark is state on the intake that the data source stores, and it's never cleared. A marked intake adds nothing anywhere in the window, so marking only saves work: the curve is the same whether or not an intake has been marked yet.
- **Features observe the curve.** `ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCase` streams the curve to features (constitution Article I.4). There's no use case for the calculation itself: nothing asks for a calculation on its own, and features only need the curve.
- **The repository recalculates on refresh, not on a timer.** It recalculates when the data the curve comes from changes, and when a feature starts observing, which happens each time a view appears. The window reaches 12 hours ahead, so a curve stays usable for up to 12 hours after it's calculated. There's no need to recalculate 1,440 levels every minute.
- **Presentation chooses what to show.** Each feature shows the whole window or a part of it, such as the next few hours. It reads the current level from the sample for the current minute, found by date. That's the middle sample when the curve is calculated, and it moves later in the window as time passes. A feature can move its "now" marker along the curve it already has without asking for a new one. It never evaluates the decay function itself.
- **Bateman absorption replaces the rule's implementation.** The repository, the use case, and the entities keep working without changes.

The rule has three operations:

| Operation | Takes | Returns |
|-----------|-------|---------|
| Level | Intakes, a half-life, and a moment `t` | The `CaffeineLevel` at `t` |
| Curve | Intakes, a half-life, and the current time | `[CaffeineLevel]`: 1,440 levels, one per minute, across the window |
| Negligible intakes | Intakes, a half-life, and the current time | The intakes that are negligible at the window's first sample |

The Level and Curve operations leave out negligible intakes (see "Dropping negligible intakes" above).

The curve always holds 1,440 levels, however many intakes are counting. That's about 23 KB at 16 bytes per level. If the size ever becomes a problem, the spacing constant can be widened.

The repository publishes the **active curve**: the window around the current time. Past-day curves, like the Patterns screen's, will read intakes through a separate path to the data source that includes marked ones. That path is designed separately.
...
| RULE-7 | The curve's samples are exactly one minute apart, on whole clock minutes. The spacing and the window are constants. |
```

````
## Subsystem and categories

Every logger uses one subsystem, the app's bundle identifier `com.quillanq.Half-Life`. Its category is the name of the type that owns it. A developer can filter to the whole app by subsystem, or to one type by category, and the category shows where a message came from without searching the source.

Types don't call `Logger(subsystem:category:)` themselves. A single extension applies the convention (a sketch until it's built):

```swift
import OSLog

extension Logger {
    /// Creates a logger in Half-Life's subsystem, categorized by the type that owns it.
    init<Owner>(for owner: Owner.Type) {
        self.init(subsystem: "com.quillanq.Half-Life", category: String(describing: owner))
    }
}
```

Each type that logs keeps one private logger, named `logger`. The SwiftLint rules look for calls on `logger`:

```swift
actor LiveCaffeineDecayRepository: CaffeineDecayRepository {
    private static let logger = Logger(for: LiveCaffeineDecayRepository.self)
}
```

## Where logging happens

| Layer | Logs? | What it logs |
|-------|-------|--------------|
| Presentation: reducers | Yes | Failures that use cases throw, caught in a `.run` effect's `catch:`, plus feature transitions a bug report needs |
| Presentation: views | No | Views send actions, and the reducer logs |
| Domain | No | Domain imports only Swift and Foundation (Article I), and `Foundation` doesn't provide `Logger`. Domain reports failures by throwing, and the code that catches the error logs it. |
| Data: repositories | Yes | State transitions and failures reported by data sources |
| Data: data sources | Yes | Framework failures, with the error's domain and code |

Importing only `Foundation` and calling `Logger` fails with "cannot find 'Logger' in scope". This was checked against the iOS 26.5 SDK on 2026-09-11.

TCA's `._printChanges()` is for local debugging only and is never committed. It prints every `State` change, health values included, through `print` rather than `Logger`.

## Choosing a level
````

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
```

```
// Half-LifeTests SwiftDataDrinkLogDataSourceTests
//

import Foundation
import SwiftData
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

/// Checks the SwiftData drink log against DATA-1 to DATA-5 in the Caffeine Decay Model article and SRC-1, SRC-3,
/// and SRC-4 in the Drink Composer article. Each test uses its own in-memory store, with CloudKit off.
struct SwiftDataDrinkLogDataSourceTests {

    let container: ModelContainer
    let source: SwiftDataDrinkLogDataSource

    init() throws {
        container = try SwiftDataDrinkLogDataSource.makeModelContainer(isStoredInMemoryOnly: true)
        source = SwiftDataDrinkLogDataSource(modelContainer: container)
    }

    static let midnight = Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate: 0)

    static func drink(_ type: DrinkType = .espresso, milligrams: Double = 64, hours: Double) -> LoggedDrink {
        LoggedDrink(type: type, quantity: 1, milligrams: milligrams, consumedAt: midnight.addingTimeInterval(hours * 3_600))
    }

    /// Counts the signals `changes` delivers for `work`. Signals are buffered, so after a short wait every signal
    /// the work sent has been counted, and cancelling ends the count.
    static func signalCount(_ changes: AsyncStream<Void>, during work: () async throws -> Void) async throws -> Int {
        let counter = Task {
            var count = 0
            for await _ in changes {
                count += 1
            }
            return count
        }
        try await work()
        try await Task.sleep(for: .milliseconds(200))
        counter.cancel()
        return await counter.value
    }

    @Test func storedDrinkComesBackWithEveryField() async throws {
        let drink = LoggedDrink(
            type: .coldBrew, quantity: 2, milligrams: 410, consumedAt: Self.midnight.addingTimeInterval(8 * 3_600))

        try await source.store(drink)

        #expect(try await source.drinks() == [drink])
    }

    @Test func drinksComeBackOldestFirst() async throws {
        let later = Self.drink(hours: 15)
        let earlier = Self.drink(hours: 8)

        try await source.store(later)
        try await source.store(earlier)

        #expect(try await source.drinks() == [earlier, later])
    }

    /// DATA-4.
    @Test func newDrinkIsStoredUnmarked() async throws {
        let drink = Self.drink(hours: 8)

        try await source.store(drink)

        #expect(try await source.nonNegligibleDrinks() == [drink])
    }

    /// DATA-1 and SRC-4.
    @Test func markedDrinksAreLeftOutOfTheActiveQuery() async throws {
        let old = Self.drink(hours: 0)
        let recent = Self.drink(hours: 40)
        try await source.store(old)
        try await source.store(recent)

        try await source.markNegligible([old.intake])

        #expect(try await source.nonNegligibleDrinks() == [recent])
    }

    /// DATA-3.
    @Test func markingNeverDeletesADrink() async throws {
        let old = Self.drink(hours: 0)
        let recent = Self.drink(hours: 40)
        try await source.store(old)
        try await source.store(recent)

        try await source.markNegligible([old.intake])

        #expect(try await source.drinks() == [old, recent])
    }

    /// DATA-2: a second data source over the same store still sees the mark.
    @Test func marksPersistInTheStore() async throws {
        let old = Self.drink(hours: 0)
        try await source.store(old)

        try await source.markNegligible([old.intake])

        let reopened = SwiftDataDrinkLogDataSource(modelContainer: container)
        #expect(try await reopened.nonNegligibleDrinks().isEmpty)
    }

    /// DATA-5 and SRC-1.
    @Test func everySubscriberGetsOneSignalPerStore() async throws {
        let first = await source.changes()
        let second = await source.changes()

        let firstCount = try await Self.signalCount(first) {
            try await source.store(Self.drink(hours: 8))
            try await source.store(Self.drink(hours: 9))
        }
        let secondCount = try await Self.signalCount(second) {}

        #expect(firstCount == 2)
        #expect(secondCount == 2)
    }

    /// DATA-5 and SRC-3.
    @Test func markingSignalsNothing() async throws {
        let old = Self.drink(hours: 0)
        try await source.store(old)
        let changes = await source.changes()

        let count = try await Self.signalCount(changes) {
            try await source.markNegligible([old.intake])
        }

        #expect(count == 0)
    }

    /// A drink whose stored type this version doesn't know, for example from a newer version synced through
    /// CloudKit, is left out rather than failing the whole read.
    @Test func drinkWithAnUnknownTypeIsSkipped() async throws {
        let context = ModelContext(container)
        context.insert(DrinkRecord(id: UUID(), typeRawValue: "unknownDrink", quantity: 1, milligrams: 50, consumedAt: .now))
        try context.save()
        let known = Self.drink(hours: 8)
        try await source.store(known)

        #expect(try await source.drinks() == [known])
        #expect(try await source.nonNegligibleDrinks() == [known])
    }
}
```

```
The build break in LiveUserProfileRepository.swift has a confirmed root cause: `nonisolated protocol UserProfileRepository`. In the same module, an actor that conforms to a `nonisolated` protocol inherits `nonisolated` (SE-0449). Actors can't be nonisolated, which gives both "'nonisolated' modifier cannot be applied to this declaration" at the actor and the warning on its init.
In a scratch copy of the live tree, changing only `nonisolated protocol UserProfileRepository` to `protocol UserProfileRepository` made the app target build. Adding `import Foundation` didn't help.
Under the target's `nonisolated` default, `nonisolated` on a protocol is redundant anyway. I'm removing it from my own `CaffeineDecayRepository` and `UseCase` protocols for the same reason, and I'll document "never write `nonisolated` on a protocol" in the Architecture article's isolation section. `UserProfileDataSource` is also `nonisolated protocol`. Its conformer is a struct, so it doesn't break, but you may want the same cleanup there.
```

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |

// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-Life CaffeineDecayRepository
//

/// The source of truth for the active caffeine curve.
///
/// An implementation reads the drinks that aren't marked negligible from the drink log data source, reads the
/// half-life from the half-life data source, and executes ``CaffeineDecayRule`` to calculate the curve. It has the
/// drink log data source mark the intakes the rule finds negligible, so later reads skip them. Drinks reach it
/// only through the data source, which signals when they change. The Caffeine Decay Model article lists its
/// requirements, REPO-1 to REPO-6.
///
/// It isn't marked `nonisolated`: an actor that conforms to a `nonisolated` protocol in the same module inherits
/// `nonisolated`, which an actor can't be (constitution Article IV.1).
protocol CaffeineDecayRepository: Sendable {
    /// Streams the active curve.
    ///
    /// Each new subscriber immediately receives a curve calculated for the current time. After that, every
    /// subscriber receives a new curve whenever the data the curve comes from changes. The repository never
    /// recalculates on a timer.
    func curve() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]>
}
```

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |

//
// Half-LifeTests FakeCaffeineDecayRepository
//

import Foundation

@testable import Half_Life

/// An in-memory caffeine decay repository for use case tests.
///
/// It streams the curves it was given, then finishes. It's an actor, like the live repositories.
actor FakeCaffeineDecayRepository: CaffeineDecayRepository {
    /// The curves that `curve()` streams, in order.
    let curves: [[CaffeineLevel]]

    init(curves: [[CaffeineLevel]] = []) {
        self.curves = curves
    }

    nonisolated func curve() -> AsyncStream<[CaffeineLevel]> {
        AsyncStream { continuation in
            for curve in curves {
                continuation.yield(curve)
            }
            continuation.finish()
        }
    }
}
```

- `content`:

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-LifeTests FakeDrinkLogDataSource
//

import Foundation

@testable import Half_Life

/// An in-memory drink log data source for repository tests.
///
/// It holds drinks and their negligible marks, and records what repositories ask of it. Like the live data source,
/// `store(_:)` signals a change and `markNegligible(_:)` doesn't. A test can also insert a drink without a signal,
/// then call `signalChange()` to control exactly when repositories hear about it.
actor FakeDrinkLogDataSource: DrinkLogDataSource {
    /// Every drink held, in the order it was added.
    private(set) var heldDrinks: [LoggedDrink]
    /// The identifiers of the drinks marked negligible.
    private(set) var negligibleIDs: Set<UUID>
    /// The intakes passed to each `markNegligible(_:)` call, in order.
    private(set) var markedIntakes: [[CaffeineIntake]] = []
    /// How many times `drinks()` was called.
    private(set) var drinksCallCount = 0
    /// How many times `nonNegligibleDrinks()` was called.
    private(set) var nonNegligibleDrinksCallCount = 0
    /// The error `store(_:)` throws instead of storing, if any.
    private let storeError: (any Error)?
    private var subscribers: [UUID: AsyncStream<Void>.Continuation] = [:]

    init(drinks: [LoggedDrink] = [], negligibleIDs: Set<UUID> = [], storeError: (any Error)? = nil) {
        heldDrinks = drinks
        self.negligibleIDs = negligibleIDs
        self.storeError = storeError
    }

    /// The number of streams from `changes()` still being listened to.
    var subscriberCount: Int {
        subscribers.count
    }

    func store(_ drink: LoggedDrink) async throws {
        if let storeError {
            throw storeError
        }
        heldDrinks.append(drink)
        signalChange()
    }

    func drinks() async throws -> [LoggedDrink] {
        drinksCallCount += 1
        return oldestFirst(heldDrinks)
    }

    func nonNegligibleDrinks() async throws -> [LoggedDrink] {
        nonNegligibleDrinksCallCount += 1
        return oldestFirst(heldDrinks.filter { !negligibleIDs.contains($0.id) })
    }

    func markNegligible(_ intakes: [CaffeineIntake]) async throws {
        markedIntakes.append(intakes)
        negligibleIDs.formUnion(intakes.map(\.id))
    }

    func changes() -> AsyncStream<Void> {
        let (stream, continuation) = AsyncStream.makeStream(of: Void.self)
        let id = UUID()
        subscribers[id] = continuation
        continuation.onTermination = { [weak self] _ in
            Task { await self?.removeSubscriber(id) }
        }
        return stream
    }

    /// Adds a drink without signalling a change.
    func insert(_ drink: LoggedDrink) {
        heldDrinks.append(drink)
    }

    /// Signals a change to every subscriber, as the live data source does after a store.
    func signalChange() {
        for subscriber in subscribers.values {
            subscriber.yield()
        }
    }

    private func removeSubscriber(_ id: UUID) {
        subscribers[id] = nil
    }

    private func oldestFirst(_ drinks: [LoggedDrink]) -> [LoggedDrink] {
        drinks.sorted { $0.consumedAt < $1.consumedAt }
    }
}

```

- `content`:

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |
// |  |_|  ||       ||   | |   |___ |   |___ |       ||  _    ||  |_|  |
// |      | |       ||   | |       ||       ||   _   || | |   ||      |
// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-LifeTests LiveCaffeineDecayRepositoryTests
//

import Foundation
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

/// Checks the live decay repository against REPO-1 to REPO-6 in the Caffeine Decay Model article, with a fake drink
/// log, a fake half-life, and a fixed clock. The time limit turns a curve that never arrives into a failure instead
/// of a hang.
@Suite(.timeLimit(.minutes(1)))
struct LiveCaffeineDecayRepositoryTests {

    let rule = CaffeineDecayRule()

    static let midnight = Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate: 0)
    /// 8:00pm on the worked examples' day.
    static let evening = date(20)

    static func date(_ hours: Double) -> Date {
        midnight.addingTimeInterval(hours * 3_600)
    }

    static func drink(_ milligrams: Double, hours: Double) -> LoggedDrink {
        LoggedDrink(type: .espresso, quantity: 1, milligrams: milligrams, consumedAt: date(hours))
    }

    /// The worked examples' day of drinks: 128 mg at 8:00am, 64 mg at 10:45am, and 205 mg at 3:00pm.
    static let dayOfDrinks = [drink(128, hours: 8), drink(64, hours: 10.75), drink(205, hours: 15)]

    static func repository(
        _ source: FakeDrinkLogDataSource, halfLife: CaffeineHalfLife = .standard, now: Date = evening
    ) -> LiveCaffeineDecayRepository {
        LiveCaffeineDecayRepository(
            drinkLog: source,
            halfLife: FakeHalfLifeDataSource(value: halfLife),
            clock: FakeClockDataSource(date: now, minuteDates: []))
    }

    // MARK: - REPO-2: a new subscriber gets a curve for the current time

    @Test func newSubscriberGetsACurveCalculatedForTheCurrentTime() async throws {
        let repository = Self.repository(FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: Self.dayOfDrinks))

        var curves = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        let curve = try #require(await curves.next())

        #expect(curve == rule.curve(from: Self.dayOfDrinks.map(\.intake), halfLife: .standard, now: Self.evening))
    }

    // MARK: - REPO-1: only drinks that aren't marked negligible

    @Test func readsOnlyTheDrinksThatArentMarkedNegligible() async throws {
        let marked = Self.drink(200, hours: 19)
        let source = FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: Self.dayOfDrinks + [marked], negligibleIDs: [marked.id])
        let repository = Self.repository(source)

        var curves = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        let curve = try #require(await curves.next())

        #expect(curve == rule.curve(from: Self.dayOfDrinks.map(\.intake), halfLife: .standard, now: Self.evening))
        #expect(await source.nonNegligibleDrinksCallCount > 0)
        #expect(await source.drinksCallCount == 0)
    }

    // MARK: - REPO-3: marks exactly the negligible intakes

    /// 64 mg stops counting after 38.5 hours. At 50.5 hours plus a minute, the window's first sample is just past it.
    @Test func marksExactlyTheIntakesNegligibleAtTheWindowsFirstSample() async throws {
        let old = Self.drink(64, hours: 0)
        let recent = Self.drink(200, hours: 40)
        let source = FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: [old, recent])
        let repository = Self.repository(source, now: Self.date(50.5).addingTimeInterval(60))

        var curves = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        _ = try #require(await curves.next())

        #expect(await source.markedIntakes == [[old.intake]])
    }

    @Test func marksNothingWhenNoIntakeIsNegligible() async throws {
        let source = FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: Self.dayOfDrinks)
        let repository = Self.repository(source)

        var curves = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        _ = try #require(await curves.next())

        #expect(await source.markedIntakes.isEmpty)
    }

    // MARK: - REPO-4: marking changes no level

    @Test func curveIsTheSameWhetherOrNotTheNegligibleIntakeIsMarked() async throws {
        let old = Self.drink(64, hours: 0)
        let recent = Self.drink(200, hours: 40)
        let now = Self.date(50.5).addingTimeInterval(60)
        let repository = Self.repository(FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: [old, recent]), now: now)

        var curves = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        let curve = try #require(await curves.next())

        let withOld = rule.curve(from: [old.intake, recent.intake], halfLife: .standard, now: now)
        let withoutOld = rule.curve(from: [recent.intake], halfLife: .standard, now: now)
        #expect(withOld == withoutOld)
        #expect(curve == withoutOld)
    }

    // MARK: - REPO-5: a change reaches every subscriber

    @Test func everySubscriberGetsARecalculatedCurveWhenTheDataSourceSignalsAChange() async throws {
        let source = FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: Self.dayOfDrinks)
        let repository = Self.repository(source)
        var first = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        var second = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        _ = try #require(await first.next())
        _ = try #require(await second.next())

        let lateCup = Self.drink(95, hours: 19)
        await source.insert(lateCup)
        await source.signalChange()

        let expected = rule.curve(
            from: (Self.dayOfDrinks + [lateCup]).map(\.intake), halfLife: .standard, now: Self.evening)
        #expect(await first.next() == expected)
        #expect(await second.next() == expected)
    }

    // MARK: - REPO-6: the data source's half-life

    @Test func calculatesWithTheHalfLifeFromTheDataSource() async throws {
        let oneHour = try #require(CaffeineHalfLife(seconds: 3_600))
        let repository = Self.repository(FakeDrinkLogDataSource(drinks: Self.dayOfDrinks), halfLife: oneHour)

        var curves = repository.curve().makeAsyncIterator()
        let curve = try #require(await curves.next())

        #expect(curve == rule.curve(from: Self.dayOfDrinks.map(\.intake), halfLife: oneHour, now: Self.evening))
    }
}

```

```
    func halfLife() async throws -> CaffeineHalfLife
}
```

```
/// The half-life data source until the tuning features can store a user's own half-life.
///
/// Nothing can store a half-life yet, so the current one is always ``CaffeineHalfLife/standard`` (HALF-1). The

struct StandardHalfLifeDataSource: HalfLifeDataSource {

    }
```

```
//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |
// |  | |  ||  |_|  ||   | |   |    |   |    |       ||       ||  | |  |

// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-Life DrinkLogDataSource
//

/// Stores the drinks the user logs, and their negligible marks.
///
/// An implementation is the only code that touches the drink log's storage (constitution Article I.14). Two
/// repositories share it. The drink log repository stores and reads drinks, and ``LiveCaffeineDecayRepository``
/// reads the drinks that still count and marks the ones that don't. The data source signals a change after each
/// store, so both repositories hear about a drink however it was stored. The Drink Composer article lists its
/// requirements, SRC-1 to SRC-4, and the Caffeine Decay Model article lists DATA-1 to DATA-5.
protocol DrinkLogDataSource: Sendable {
    /// Stores a drink, unmarked, then signals a change to every subscriber.
    ///
    /// - Parameter drink: The drink to store.
    /// - Throws: An error if the drink couldn't be stored. Nothing is signalled then.
    func store(_ drink: LoggedDrink) async throws

    /// Returns every stored drink, oldest first, whether or not it's marked negligible.
    ///
    /// - Throws: An error if the drinks couldn't be read.
    func drinks() async throws -> [LoggedDrink]

    /// Returns the drinks that aren't marked negligible, oldest first.
    ///
    /// - Throws: An error if the drinks couldn't be read.
    func nonNegligibleDrinks() async throws -> [LoggedDrink]

    /// Marks the drinks with the given intakes' identifiers as negligible.
    ///
    /// A mark is never cleared, marking never deletes a drink, and marking signals nothing.
    ///
    /// - Parameter intakes: The intakes whose drinks to mark.
    /// - Throws: An error if the marks couldn't be stored.
    func markNegligible(_ intakes: [CaffeineIntake]) async throws

    /// Returns a stream for one subscriber that yields after each change.
    ///
    /// The stream ends when its subscriber stops listening.
    func changes() async -> AsyncStream<Void>
}

```
